The invasion left massive destruction to the infrastructure, streets, [traffic light network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_light_control_and_coordination), main water lines, and caused the postponement of the Palestinian high school exam. The invasion also led to the killing of two Palestinians (a child and a woman) and the injury of others. Israel announced the killing of one of its soldiers and the injury of others during the invasion, in addition to the bombing and significant destruction of an Israeli " Tiger " vehicle. This invasion came only hours after an Israeli air strike on a house in the Nour Shams camp on Sunday afternoon, June 30, 2024, which led to the killing of "Saeed Jaber", one of the leaders of the Tulkarm Brigade.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429]

The invasion began with the destruction by Israeli bulldozers of the Al-Younis Roundabout area in Tulkarm, followed by the destruction of Al-Sikka Street and Iktaba Roundabout in the city. After that, the Israeli forces and their bulldozers moved to Nablus Street in the city, which they largely destroyed. The Israeli army once again razed what remained of the "Saif Roundabout" area after completely destroying it twice in March 2024.[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431]

The Israeli forces then penetrated deeper into the Nour Shams camp, causing violent clashes to erupt with the Tulkarm Brigade detonated explosive devices, one of which hit an Israeli armored vehicle, destroying it "significantly" and injuring those inside.[^cite_note-432] This prompted the Israeli army to bring in a heavy military logistical vehicle to tow the damaged vehicle.[^cite_note-433] The Israeli army also transferred its wounded soldiers from Tulkarm to the Israeli Sna'oz camp adjacent to the city, followed by the landing of an Israeli military helicopter in the camp, transporting the wounded soldiers. On the same day, Israel announced the death of one of its soldiers, 22-year-old Major Yehuda Gato, the driver of the vehicle, and the serious injury of another as a result of the explosion, all of whom belonged to the [Duvdevan unit](11117982.md) as part of the [Oz Brigade](49096690.md).[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

At noon, the intensity of armed clashes between Tulkarm Brigade members and the Israeli army escalated at the entrance to Tulkarm camp as Israeli vehicles and bulldozers surrounded the entrance to the camp, which led to the killing of two Palestinians (a child and a woman) in Tulkarm camp by Israeli army bullets. Several Palestinians were also injured in the city's neighborhoods and camps, as announced by the director of Tulkarm Governmental Hospital and the Palestinian Ministry of Health.[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439] In addition, Israeli bulldozers destroyed the traffic light network in the Zenobia Junction area in the center of the city at noon.[^cite_note-440]

The [Palestinian Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Ministry_of_Education) had announced at eight o'clock a.m. the postponement of the unified Palestinian high school exam ([English language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) exam) in the morning for all schools in Tulkarm Governorate due to the ongoing Israeli invasion of Tulkarm, which was planned to be administered that morning.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442]

Hours prior to this, Israel had bombed a house in the city's Nour Shams camp on Sunday afternoon, June 30, 2024, with three missiles from an aircraft. The missiles killed Saeed Jaber, the leader of the Tulkarm Brigade, and wounded five other Palestinians who were taken to the Tulkarm Governmental Hospital, with two in critical condition.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444]

A few hours after the Israeli withdrawal, the Israeli army bombed the Nour Shams camp with a drone on the evening of Tuesday, July 2, 2024, instantly killing four Palestinians.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446]

#### July 9, 2024 incursion

Destroyed section of a street in front of two buildings

Destruction caused by the [IDF](39237.md) in Nablus Street and Nour Shams Camp, [Tulkarm](851834.md), in July 2024

On Tuesday, July 9, 2024, Israel invaded the city of Tulkarm and the city's Nour Shams camp with dozens of military vehicles accompanied by seven military bulldozers. The invasion continued until around afternoon on the same day. The invasion left immense destruction in the streets and infrastructure. The destruction was described as the largest in the history of the West Bank since [Israel's occupation](59076253.md) of the territory in 1967.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448]

The invasion began with the Israeli army completely besieging both the government hospital and the Israa Specialized Hospital in the city center. The army deployed its vehicles in the commercial market area in the city center, checked the identities of journalists while they were covering the events, and deployed its snipers in several buildings. The army also besieged the Tulkarm camp in the city, and caused significant disruption to the city's communications and [internet networks](100245.md).[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450]

Four military bulldozers worked collectively to completely destroy the Younis Roundabout square in the middle of the city, which led to the disappearance of its features and the destruction of its infrastructure.[^cite_note-451] The bulldozers, including the excavator, swept away Nablus Street in the area adjacent to the Nour Shams camp, turning it into a trench more than three meters deep and completely destroying it in addition to shops, homes, electricity, communications, internet, sewage, and water lines.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] The bulldozers also destroyed the Saif Roundabout square area and the surrounding shops, destroying it completely for the fourth time since March 2024. At the same time, the bulldozers penetrated deep into the Nour Shams camp and destroyed several homes and streets in it.[^cite_note-454]

Following the Israeli withdrawal, the Tulkarm Municipality announced that "Nour Shams Camp in Tulkarm City is officially considered a [disaster area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disaster_area)".[^cite_note-455] The National Action Movement announced that the city of Tulkarm and its two camps were now a disaster area in need of urgent reconstruction and consular visits.[^cite_note-456]

The Palestinian government condemned what it described as Israeli aggression on Tulkarm and the unprecedented destruction of the Nour Shams camp, the infrastructure, and the main electricity and water lines in the city.[^cite_note-457] The National Union of Civil Society Organizations in Tulkarm Governorate also called on the Palestinian government to urgently invite consuls and international institutions to see the extent of the destruction brought on the city.[^cite_note-458]

The Minister of Local Government, Sami Hijjawi, the Minister of Public Works, Ahed Bseiso, and the Director General of Civil Defense, Al-Abed Ibrahim Khalil, inspected the extent of the destruction in Nour Shams camp.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] Palestinian governmental and civil institutions announced that Israel had completely or partially destroyed dozens of Palestinian homes in the Nour Shams camp.[^cite_note-461]

#### July 22–23, 2024 incursion

Israel invaded the city of Tulkarm and the Tulkarm refugee camp in the city at approximately 10\:45 p.m. on July 22, 2024, with dozens of Israeli military vehicles and seven military bulldozers.[^cite_note-\:18-462] The invasion continued until approximately 1\:30 p.m. on July 23, and resulted in massive destruction, the "widest and most severe" to the infrastructure and streets in the center of the city and the Tulkarm camp. The devastation also led to the interruption of electricity, communications, the Internet, and water for long hours in many of the city's neighborhoods, including Tulkarm camp. Five Palestinians were killed and many others were seriously injured.[^cite_note-\:19-463][^cite_note-464]

In Tulkarm camp, the invasion of the camp began with the Israeli army entering the city from its western and southern axes.[^cite_note-465] Israeli bulldozers destroyed the infrastructure and streets in the camp, and an Israeli drone bombed the camp, killing five Palestinians that including two commanders in the Tulkarm Brigade.[^cite_note-466] The Israeli army spokesman said in a brief statement that "an [air force](177619.md) plane attacked gunmen during the military operation" and the Tulkarm Brigade announced that it had detonated several explosive devices, one of which hit an Israeli bulldozer and damaged it, which prompted the Israeli army to drag it out of Tulkarm using another bulldozer.[^cite_note-\:19-463][^cite_note-467]

In the city center, Israeli bulldozers razed part of Government Hospital Street in addition to Muqata'a Street and two Israeli tracked bulldozers destroyed the vegetable market in the city center and the vicinity of Gamal Abdel Nasser Square.[^cite_note-\:19-463]

In the western neighborhoods of the city, two Israeli bulldozers destroyed the electricity, communications, and internet networks, poles, and cables on Yaffa Street, Al-Fadhiliyah Street, Khadouri Roundabout, and Hilmi Hanoun School Street, and also demolished part of the wall of Hilmi Hanoun School, while crushing nearby parked vehicles and uprooting trees.[^cite_note-\:18-462][^cite_note-\:19-463]

In the eastern neighborhoods of the city, Israeli bulldozers destroyed Muqata'a Street, destroyed the Martyrs' Monument, demolished part of the wall of the Local Government and Public Works Directorates, bulldozed the Abu Safiya Junction Street along with other streets, and destroyed several parked vehicles.[^cite_note-\:19-463][^cite_note-468]

The Israeli army described the invasion as a military operation in a series of counter-terrorism operations in Tulkarm, while Palestinian Prime Minister Muhammad Mustafa condemned what he called a "brutal attack" on Tulkarm during the weekly government session on the morning of July 23, 2024.[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470]

### August 2024

#### August 3, 2024 incursion

At approximately 4\:50 am on Saturday, August 3, 2024, the Israeli army entered accompanied by bulldozers and completely destroyed the "Al-Alimi Roundabout" (the Palestinian flagpole square) in the city and its surroundings, leading to its comprehensive destruction. The Israeli army then withdrew from the city at approximately 5\:50 a.m., killing five Palestinians north of the governorate in an airstrike.[^cite_note-471] The Israeli army then returned at 10\:00 a.m and invaded the city in a more widespread area, including the Tulkarm camp. The invasion continued until 2\:00 p.m., during which the Israeli army killed four more Palestinians in a new airstrike east of the city and bringing the Palestinian death toll to nine, in addition to massive destruction.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473]

This invasion led to great destruction in the western and eastern neighborhoods of the city and the Tulkarm camp in the center of the city. In the eastern neighborhoods, Israeli bulldozers destroyed several walls and completely smashed many vehicles. In the western neighborhoods, forces completely razed the Al-Alimi Roundabout and its surroundings. In the Tulkarm camp, bulldozers again razed the streets of the camp, leaving great destruction amidst violent clashes.[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475]

#### August 28, 2024 incursion

Main article: [2024 Israeli military operation in the northern West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_military_operation_in_the_northern_West_Bank)

Israel launched a large-scale military operation into both Tulkarm and [Jenin](50997.md) involving hundreds of IDF soldiers.[^cite_note-\:022-476][^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478]

### January 2025

On 27 January 2025, Israel formally expanded its "[Iron Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Israeli_military_operation_in_the_West_Bank)" military operation into Tulkarm.[^cite_note-iran-update-january-27-2025-479][^cite_note-france24-20250203-480]

## Other raids and incursions

### Ramallah

On 12 October 2023, a group of Israeli soldiers and settlers stripped a group of men naked in [Ramallah](44132.md), urinating on them, burning them with cigarettes, and sexually assaulted one man.[^cite_note-481] Five people were wounded during a raid in [Ramallah](44132.md).[^cite_note-482] On 5 January, a teenage boy was shot and killed in [Bani Zeid al-Gharbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bani_Zeid_al-Gharbia).[^cite_note-483] A local doctor was blindfolded and arrested during a Ramallah raid on 7 January.[^cite_note-484] A doctor and nurse were arrested in Ramallah on 8 January.[^cite_note-485] Israeli forces reportedly released poisonous gas during a raid in Beit Rima on 28 January.[^cite_note-486]

On 7 February 2024, Israeli forces broke into the house of an American woman in [Silwad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silwad), pulled her out of bed and arrested her due to her social media posts.[^cite_note-487] A teenager was reportedly shot in the head and killed by Israeli forces on 10 February.[^cite_note-488] Six children were reportedly arrested in Aboud on 20 February.[^cite_note-489] Two ten-year-olds were arrested on 22 February.[^cite_note-490] The body of a 13-year-old boy from [Jalazone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalazone) was found dead near the separation wall, close to [Beit El](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_El), an illegal Israeli settlement.[^cite_note-491] A teenage boy was shot dead by Israeli forces on 2 March in [Kafr Ni'ma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafr_Ni'ma).[^cite_note-492] A 13-year-old boy was killed by Israeli forces on 3 March, after he was reportedly left bleeding out for hours.[^cite_note-493] A 16-year-old protester was shot and killed on 4 March.[^cite_note-494] In June 2024, Israeli forces raided [Kafr Nima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafr_Nima), killing four people and wounding four.[^cite_note-495] A 12-year-old boy died after being shot by Israeli forces in June 2024.[^cite_note-496]

#### Killing of Tawfiq Ajaq

Main article: [Killing of Tawfic Abdel Jabbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Tawfic_Abdel_Jabbar)

On 19 January 2024, Israeli forces killed an American teenager in [Al-Mazra'a ash-Sharqiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mazra'a_ash-Sharqiya).[^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498][^cite_note-500] His father stated, "They are killer machines. They are using our tax dollars in the US to support the weapons to kill our own children."[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] The U.S. Office for Palestinian Affairs called for an investigation into the boy's death.[^cite_note-503] The [U.S. State Department](31975.md) stated, "We are working to understand the circumstances of the incident \[and] have asked the Government of Israel for further information."[^cite_note-504] The [National Security Council](44026.md) spokesman stated the White House was "seriously concerned about these reports".[^cite_note-505] The State Department called for an "urgent investigation" on 22 January.[^cite_note-506] On 25 January, U.S. Congressmember [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib) called for an investigation into Ajaq's death, stating, "Tawfiq deserved to grow old."[^cite_note-507] A witness of the killing stated the shooting was unprovoked.[^cite_note-508]

A month later on 10 February, Israeli forces killed another American citizen in [Biddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biddu,_Jerusalem). The victim was 17-year-old [Mohammad Khdour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Khdour), who was shot in the head while out driving.[^cite_note-509][^cite_note-510]

### Hebron

Main article: [Israeli–Palestinian conflict in Hebron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict_in_Hebron)

An investigation was launched on 6 December 2023 after Israeli soldiers shot and wounded a mentally disabled man in [Hebron](38577.md).[^cite_note-511] A 28-year-old pharmaceutical representative was shot dead by the IDF in [Beit Einun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Einun) after turning on a road he didn't know was closed.[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513] On 21 December 2023, Israeli forces raided multiple areas across the Palestinian territories including [Halhul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halhul) and [Surif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surif).[^cite_note-514] On 27 December, the Palestinian Red Crescent reported three people were wounded during an IDF raid in Hebron.[^cite_note-515] People in Hebron were injured on 30 December 2023 with rubber bullets and tear gas.[^cite_note-516] On 30 December 2023, the governor of the [Hebron Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebron_Governorate) stated the city of Hebron had been sealed off by the IDF since 7 October.[^cite_note-517] On 29 December 2023, Israeli forces assaulted and fired shots at Palestinian shepherds in the [Masafer Yatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masafer_Yatta) region.[^cite_note-518]

Raids were reported in [Al-Arroub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Arroub) on 5 January 2024.[^cite_note-519] On 13 January 2024, Israeli forces shot and killed three teenage boys near [Adora, Har Hevron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adora,_Har_Hevron).[^cite_note-520] Later, troops raided the boys' family house in Hebron, using tear gas, sound bombs, and rubber bullets, while their family was mourning the killings.[^cite_note-521] On 15 January 2024, a young man and young woman were killed near Hebron after an Israeli raid.[^cite_note-522] On 19 January 2024, the home of Imad Abu Shamsieh, a human rights activist, was raided, and he was told to stop "filming and documenting".[^cite_note-523] On 29 January 2024, the acting mayor of Hebron said Israeli forces had killed two young men and wounded three in [Dura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dura,_Hebron).[^cite_note-524] A boy killed on his way home from school on 15 February 2024 was the 100th child in the West Bank to be killed by Israel since 7 October.[^cite_note-525] Three young brothers were shot by Israeli forces near Hebron on 29 February while picking plants, killing one.[^cite_note-526]

On 25 March 2024, authenticated footage showed Israeli soldiers removing a young boy's shirt and slapping him in the face.[^cite_note-527] A 14-year-old boy was reportedly shot and killed in May 2024.[^cite_note-528] In August 2024, Doctors Without Borders described increased Israeli restrictions and violence against Palestinians was impacting Hebron residents physical and mental health.[^cite_note-529] During a raid on the Al-Fawar refugee camp, Israeli soldiers abused civilians and arrested more than 30 people.[^cite_note-530]

### Tubas, Bethlehem, and Qalqilya

On 15 December 2023, Israel launched an investigation after [B'tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'tselem) released video of IDF soldiers shooting two unarmed men at point-blank range in [Far'a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far'a).[^cite_note-531] Seven were wounded in a raid near [Tubas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tubas_\(city\)).[^cite_note-532] On 27 February, the acting governor of [Tubas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tubas_Governorate) stated that an Israeli raid had destroyed roads, generators, while "three young men were killed in cold blood while living their normal lives at their homes".[^cite_note-533] In June 2024, a raid on [Far'a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far'a) reportedly lasted more than 12 hours, killing a 15-year-old boy.[^cite_note-534] In September 2024, an Israeli sniper killed Sufyan Jaber Abed Jawwad, the first UN employee to be killed in the West Bank in more than a decade.[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536]

Raids were also reported in [Bethlehem](4312.md) on Christmas Day.[^cite_note-537] On 6 January, Israeli forces reportedly conducted a "crackdown" on resistance and dissent, with troops reportedly beating three men in Bethlehem.[^cite_note-538] A health clinic near Bethlehem was raided on 10 January.[^cite_note-539] Raids were reported near Bethlehem on 18 January, with four young men reportedly beaten by soldiers.[^cite_note-540] On 11 February 2024, the [Palestinian Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Ministry_of_Health) reported that a 35-year-old man had been killed by Israeli forces with 20 bullets found in his body.[^cite_note-541]

On 29 December 2023, a 17-year-old boy in [Qalqilya](851829.md) was shot in the chest and then arrested while ambulances sought to aid him.[^cite_note-542] On 2 January 2024, paramedics in [Qalqilya](851829.md) were shot when they tried to rescue an injured man.[^cite_note-543] A 20-year-old was killed when Israeli forces shot him in the head, shoulders, and chest.[^cite_note-544] A fifteen-year-old boy was killed on 21 February.[^cite_note-545] On 9 March, the mayor of [Azzun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azzun) stated that Israeli forces had killed 8 young men and arrested more than 100 since 7 October.[^cite_note-546] A 15-year-old boy was reportedly shot and killed by Israeli forces in August 2024.[^cite_note-547]

### Nablus

Explosions and armed fighting were reported in [Balata Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balata_Camp) on 24 December.[^cite_note-548] Thirty-five people were arrested in raids across the West Bank on 25 December.[^cite_note-549] 200 homes in [Burqa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burqa,_Nablus) were raided on Christmas Day.[^cite_note-550] Officials in [Sebastia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastia,_Nablus) reported Israeli settlers were attacking and destroying archeological sites.[^cite_note-551] At least 14 people were injured during a raid in Nablus on 9 January.[^cite_note-552] On 10 January, five were wounded in Nablus.[^cite_note-553] The head of the [Balata Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balata_Camp) service committee stated Israeli soldiers had killed five men in the camp on 17 January 2024, then stated, "Israeli forces kidnapped the bodies of four of the men as well as some body parts of Yazan al-Najmi".[^cite_note-554] On 4 February, Israeli forces released a military dog on a 4-year-old boy, who was repeatedly bit and bled profusely for three minutes before being hospitalized.[^cite_note-555] On 6 February, a young man in [Beit Furik](14896389.md) was shot and killed after Israeli forces prevented ambulances from reaching him.[^cite_note-556]

On 13 February, settlers attacked shepherds, pelting them with stones, setting a vehicle on fire, and attempting to steal their flock.[^cite_note-557] On 18 February, the governor of Nablus stated 48 people had been killed by Israelis since 7 October, and that the military was erecting gates and checkpoints, effectively turning it into a "big prison".[^cite_note-558] The village council chair of [Duma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duma,_Nablus) stated their town had been sealed off by the IDF for 90 days and required international intervention.[^cite_note-559] A 17-year-old boy was shot in the back and killed during a military raid on 29 February.[^cite_note-560] An 11-year-old was killed after being shot in the head by Israeli live ammunition in [Burin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burin,_Nablus) on 4 March.[^cite_note-561] After Israeli settlers attacked a home, Israeli forces arrived to protect the settlers, shooting live bullets and tear gas canisters at the Palestinian homeowners.[^cite_note-562] In August 2024, a Palestinian Red Crescent paramedic died a week after sustaining injuries during an Israeli raid.[^cite_note-563]

In November 2024, an Israeli soldier on a military jeep shot and killed an 11-year-old boy.[^cite_note-564]

### Across West Bank

On 31 October, Israeli forces heightened their operations in other parts of the West Bank, resulting in the deaths of five Palestinians in [Qabatiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qabatiya), [Tammun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammun), and [Nablus](233369.md). This brought the total number of casualties in the West Bank since 7 October to 90 people, according to the [Palestinian Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Ministry_of_Health).[^cite_note-iaskt-79]

The [Palestinian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Red_Crescent) reported Israeli forces had fired live ammunition on youth protesters in [Halhul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halhul), injuring two, with additional raids in [Tubas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tubas_\(city\)), [Qalqilya](851829.md), [Deir Abu Da'if](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Abu_Da'if), [Jalbun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalbun), and [Jalqamus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalqamus).[^cite_note-565] On 23 December, raids were reported across the West Bank, including in [Dheisheh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dheisheh), [Hebron](38577.md), [Yatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yatta,_Hebron), [Beita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beita,_Nablus), [Qaryut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qaryut), [Jericho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jericho), [Ein as-Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ein_as-Sultan_camp), and [Aqabat Jaber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aqabat_Jaber).[^cite_note-566] The same day, Israeli soldiers shot a man with special needs carrying a bag of lollipops reportedly for no apparent reason.[^cite_note-567] On 25 December, Israeli forces destroyed an agricultural facility in [Salfit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salfit) and ordered the residents of [Yatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yatta,_Hebron) to cease construction of a new school.[^cite_note-568] On 28 December, the [Palestine Monetary Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Monetary_Authority) reported Israeli forces raided the headquarters of six exchange companies and seized vast sums of money.[^cite_note-569] Approximately \$2.5 million were seized after soldiers blew up the bank vaults.[^cite_note-570] Raids were reported across the West Bank on 9 January.[^cite_note-571]

Five Palestinians were killed throughout the West Bank on 14 January.[^cite_note-572] Raids were reported across the West Bank on 18 January.[^cite_note-573] Raids were reported across the West Bank on 21 January, placing economic pressure on residents and disrupting peoples' livelihoods.[^cite_note-574] Raids were reported across the West Bank on 25 January.[^cite_note-575] Raids on 29 January killed five boys and young men in just 24 hours.[^cite_note-576] A Jericho raid by Israeli forces on 21 March killed a young Palestinian man.[^cite_note-577]

On 15 March 2026, Israeli soldiers [killed](82697148.md) 4 members of a Palestinian family, including two children aged 5 and 7, in Tammun. Each of the deceased were shot in the head.[^cite_note-578] Two surviving children stated that Israeli soldiers had shouted "We killed dogs" after shooting their family members.[^cite_note-579]

### East Jerusalem

On 22 December, two Palestinians in [East Jerusalem](343508.md) were wounded after Israeli forces fired [sound bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_bomb) and [tear gas](1202646.md).[^cite_note-580] On 29 December, Israeli forces fired [skunk water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skunk_water), tear gas, and rubber bullets at worshippers in [Wadi al-Joz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadi_al-Joz).[^cite_note-581] The same day, one individual was reported injured following an Israeli raid in [Kafr 'Aqab](18080956.md).[^cite_note-582] A raid was reported in [Silwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silwan), with families reporting Israeli troops ransacking homes after accusing them of shooting fireworks at settlers 2 km away.[^cite_note-583] A four-year-old girl was killed by Israeli soldiers at a checkpoint on 7 January.[^cite_note-584] A man in [az-Za'ayyem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Az-Za'ayyem) was reportedly forced to demolish his own home.[^cite_note-585] A fourteen-year-old boy was shot and killed on 5 February.[^cite_note-586] A 15-year-old boy was killed on 12 February.[^cite_note-587] A 13-year-old boy was arrested on 14 February.[^cite_note-588]

An American teenager was shot in the head and killed by Israeli police in [Biddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biddu,_Jerusalem).[^cite_note-589] In a statement, the U.S. State Department said, "We urgently call for a quick, thorough, and transparent investigation, including full accountability".[^cite_note-590] On 21 February, Fakhri Abu Diab, a researcher, stated that Israeli policy had implemented an "iron fist" on Jerusalem Palestinians, thus subjecting them to [collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_punishment).[^cite_note-591]

In May 2024, the UNRWA office was closed after Israelis set fire to the building, causing "extensive damage".[^cite_note-592] [Philippe Lazzarini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Lazzarini), head of UNRWA, stated UN staff in prior months had been harassed, intimidated, and threatened with guns by Israelis.[^cite_note-593] On 13 May, [HaMoked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HaMoked) stated that Israelis had again attacked UNRWA headquarters, setting the perimeter on fire.[^cite_note-594]

#### Killing of Rami Hamdan al-Halhuli

A 13-year-old boy was shot in the chest by Israeli forces and died in East Jerusalem on 12 March.[^cite_note-595][^cite_note-596] Israeli forces said the boy had shot fireworks at them; however, video of the incident showed he had aimed the fireworks into the air.[^cite_note-597] Clashes between protesters and Israeli forces erupted the following day.[^cite_note-598] Following the child's death, Itamar Ben-Gvir stated that he saluted the troops who had killed the "terrorist".[^cite_note-599] Israel held onto the child's body, reportedly to pressure the family to reduce the large number of people mourning the boy's death in their home.[^cite_note-600] Israeli forces stated they would release the body but stated the child would need to be buried on the opposite side of the [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier) from his family.[^cite_note-601]

The boy's family described the child, stating, "He was good at school, he was smart, he helped our neighbours."[^cite_note-602] A preliminary investigation suggested the boy was shot and killed before his firework had launched.[^cite_note-603] The boy's funeral was held on 18 March.[^cite_note-604]

### Jordan Valley

Shepherds in the Jordan Valley reported arbitrary arrests and settler violence following 7 October.[^cite_note-605] Israeli forces stormed the [Ein as-Sultan camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ein_as-Sultan_camp) on 1 February 2024.[^cite_note-606]

## Large-scale Israeli operations

### Operation Summer Camps

Main article: [2024 Israeli military operation in the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_military_operation_in_the_West_Bank)

On 28 August 2024, Israel launched its largest military operation inside the West Bank since [Operation Defensive Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Defensive_Shield) in 2002,[^cite_note-nbcnews20240828-607] calling it "Operation Summer Camps",[^cite_note-608][^cite_note-609][^cite_note-610] and deployed hundreds of IDF troops together with bulldozers and [air support](600792.md).[^cite_note-611][^cite_note-612][^cite_note-613]

The stated aim of the operation was to degrade West Bank militant forces and prevent attacks on Israeli targets. Israeli foreign minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) labelled the operation a "full-fledged war" and accused [Iran](14653.md) of trying to establish an "eastern terrorist front" against Israel in the West Bank by funding and arming militants in the region.[^cite_note-ap1-614][^cite_note-nbcnews20240828-607]

As part of the military operation, Israeli forces battled Palestinian militants in and around cities across the West Bank, including Tulkarm and Jenin. Notably, the IDF managed to kill [Abu Shujaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Shujaa) and Wassem Hazem, the heads of the [Tulkarm Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulkarm_Brigade) and Hamas' Jenin branch, respectively.[^cite_note-washingtonpost20240829-615][^cite_note-\:11-616]

On 3 October 2024, Israel launched its first [airstrike on the Tulkarm refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Tulkarm_Camp_airstrike) since Operation Defensive Shield, killing 20 people including Zahi Yaser Abd al-Razeq Oufi and Ayyth Radwan, the respective heads of the local Hamas and Palestinian Islamic Jihad branches.[^cite_note-\:2-617]

Ultimately, the operation did not deal a substantial blow to Palestinian militancy.[^cite_note-618][^cite_note-619]

### Iron Wall

Main article: [Iron Wall (Israeli military operation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Wall_\(Israeli_military_operation\))

On 21 January 2025, Israel began another large-scale military operation in the West Bank, just days after the implementation of the [January 2025 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire). Israel's operation, which it calls "Iron Wall", began in [Jenin](50997.md),[^cite_note-middleeasteye20250121-620] but has spread to [Tulkarm](851834.md) and other Palestinian cities and towns. It marks a strategically distinct and more aggressive approach against West Bank militancy compared to previous Israeli raids,[^cite_note-wsj20250203-621] and also marks the first time that the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) directly participates in an Israeli military operation.[^cite_note-middleeasteye20250122-622][^cite_note-\:israelblocks-623]

Iron Wall has been reportedly yielding operational success.[^cite_note-\:23-624][^cite_note-625][^cite_note-\:1-626] Israeli forces have seized control of the [Jenin refugee camp](23460648.md), [Nur Shams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nur_Shams), and the [refugee camp in Tulkarm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulkarm_Camp), clearing them from militants.[^cite_note-\:1-626] In August 2025, Katz stated the IDF would remain deployed to refugee camps in the northern West Bank at least until the end of the year.[^cite_note-627]

### Operation Five Stones

Main article: [Operation Five Stones](81714388.md)

On 26 November 2025, Israel launched "Operation Five Stones", which targeted Palestinian militants in an area of [Tubas Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tubas_Governorate) dubbed the "Pentagon of Villages": Tubas, Tammun, the al-Fara'a camp, [Aqaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/'Aqqaba), and [Tayasir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tayasir).[^cite_note-628][^cite_note-\:12-629] The military operation imposed a siege on Tubas Governorate.[^cite_note-630] While some sources described the operation as an extension of Iron Wall,[^cite_note-631][^cite_note-\:24-632][^cite_note-633] the IDF said it was not and referred to it as a new and distinct operation.[^cite_note-\:32-634]

After days of raids, Israeli forces pulled out of Tubas Governorate on 29 November, marking the operation's conclusion.[^cite_note-635][^cite_note-636]

## Reprisal attacks by militants

### 2023

On 20 November, the Salfit Battalion, an unaffiliated minor Palestinian militia, attacked two settler vehicles near [Az-Zawiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Az-Zawiya).[^cite_note-637]

On 21 December, militants attacked the [Beitar Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beitar_Illit) settlement near Bethlehem with explosives.[^cite_note-638]

### 2024

On 12 January, the IDF killed three militants who attempted to infiltrate an Israeli settlement near Hebron and wounded a soldier.[^cite_note-639][^cite_note-640] On 15 January, two militants who entered Israel covertly from Hebron carried out [vehicle-ramming attacks in Ra'anana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Ra'anana_attack), killing one person and injuring 18 others.[^cite_note-641]

On 16 February, the al-Quds Brigades fired at Israeli forces stationed at the Dotan checkpoint, south of the [Mevo Dotan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevo_Dotan) settlement in the West Bank.[^cite_note-642] On 29 February, three militants, including a Palestinian Authority policeman, opened fire at a gas station near the West Bank settlement of [Eli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_\(Israeli_settlement\)), killing two Israelis.[^cite_note-643]

On 16 March, a militant reportedly affiliated with Hamas opened fire on a settlement near Hebron and was killed by the IDF.[^cite_note-644]

On 13 April, the Al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades attacked an IDF checkpoint, an Israeli settlement near Tulkarm, and an Israeli town on the West Bank border.[^cite_note-645]

On 19 June, the Al-Quds Brigades targeted an outpost on the site of the former [Homesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homesh) settlement with explosives.[^cite_note-646] On 22 June, the Tulkarm Brigade[^cite_note-washingtonpost20240829-615][^cite_note-647] killed an Israeli civilian in Qalqilya.[^cite_note-648][^cite_note-649]

On 16 July, at the Ramin Junction near Nablus, militants opened fire on an Israeli vehicle that had previously injured several Palestinian civilians. Three settlers inside were wounded.[^cite_note-650][^cite_note-aljazeera20240716-53] On 31 July, a Hamas militant shot and stabbed a settler in his vehicle near [Kiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Arba), a settlement near Hebron.[^cite_note-651][^cite_note-652]

On 6 August, a militant carried out a shooting attack in the [Beka'ot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beka'ot) settlement.[^cite_note-653] On 11 August, Hamas carried out a drive-by shooting on two settlers on [Route 90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highway_90_\(Israel%E2%80%93Palestine\)) near the [Mehola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehola) settlement in the Jordan Valley, killing one and wounding the other.[^cite_note-654][^cite_note-655] On 18 August, a militant killed an Israeli security guard with a hammer near the [Kedumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kedumim) settlement.[^cite_note-656] That same day, a militant from Nablus carried out [a suicide bombing in Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_Street_bombing), wounding one person.[^cite_note-657][^cite_note-658] On 30 August, militants of the al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades[^cite_note-659] used two car bombs to attack Israeli settlements near Hebron.[^cite_note-660]

On 1 September, the Khalil al-Rahman Brigade killed three Israeli police officers who were driving near Hebron.[^cite_note-661][^cite_note-662][^cite_note-663] On 8 September, a pro-Palestinian [Jordanian](7515964.md) gunman killed three Israeli personnel at the [Allenby Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allenby_Bridge), at the border between Jordan and the West Bank.[^cite_note-664] On 11 September, a militant carried out a ramming attack near the [Givat Asaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Givat_Asaf) settlement, killing one soldier.[^cite_note-665] On 19 September, the al-Quds Brigades targeted the [Shaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaked) settlement with gunfire.[^cite_note-666] The al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades targeted two IDF checkpoints at [Mount Ebal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Ebal) and [Mount Gerzim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Gerizim) with gunfire on 19 September and 21 September, respectively.[^cite_note-667]

On 1 October, two Hamas gunmen from Hebron carried out [a shooting attack in Jaffa, Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Jaffa_shooting), killing seven people and injuring 17 others.[^cite_note-668][^cite_note-669][^cite_note-670] On 6 October, the [Mujahideen Brigades](77793730.md) of the [Palestinian Mujahideen Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Mujahideen_Movement) carried out a shooting attack on a gathering of IDF soldiers and settlers near Jenin.[^cite_note-671] On 8 October, the al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades targeted an IDF checkpoint at Mount Gerzim with gunfire.[^cite_note-672] On 11 October, the al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades targeted an IDF checkpoint at [Qalandiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qalandiya) with an explosive, and fired at the [Avnei Hefetz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avnei_Hefetz) settlement near Tulkarm.[^cite_note-673] On 19 October, PIJ targeted a vehicle in the [Mevo Dotan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevo_Dotan) settlement with gunfire.[^cite_note-iran-update-october-20-2024-674] On the same day, a militant conducted a ramming attack against an Israeli police vehicle near the [Ofra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofra) settlement, but was shot dead and did not inflict any casualties.[^cite_note-iran-update-october-20-2024-674][^cite_note-675] PIJ conducted shooting attacks targeting IDF [observation posts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observation_post) in Mevo Dotan on 24 October and a checkpoint at the Israel–West Bank border on 25 October.[^cite_note-676] A PIJ militant targeted the [Shaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaked) settlement with gunfire on 28 October.[^cite_note-iran-update-october-29-2024-58]

On 1 November, PIJ militants shot at Israeli forces near the Israel–West Bank border crossing.[^cite_note-677] On 6 November, a militant attacked and injured two Israelis at the [Shilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shilo_\(Israeli_settlement\)) settlement.[^cite_note-678] On 11 November, a militant conducted a car-ramming attack against two IDF soldiers at a checkpoint near Bethlehem.[^cite_note-679] On 29 November, a Hamas militant opened fire on an Israeli bus near the [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(Israeli_settlement\)) settlement, injuring eight people.[^cite_note-680]

On 7 December, a militant conducted a car-ramming attack near Hebron, injuring an IDF soldier and another Israeli.[^cite_note-681] On 11 December, a militant carried out a shooting attack against an Israeli bus in [al-Khader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Khader) near Bethlehem, killing one Israeli child and injuring several others.[^cite_note-682]

### 2025

On 6 January, militants [killed three Israelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_al-Funduq_shooting) near the Kedumim settlement.[^cite_note-683]

On 4 February, a Palestinian gunman attacked an Israeli checkpoint in Tayasir, killing two IDF soldiers and wounding another eight.[^cite_note-\:palgunman-684][^cite_note-685]

On 8 March, militants targeted an Israeli civilian vehicle traveling near [Nabi Ilyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nabi_Ilyas) with [Molotov cocktails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molotov_cocktails).[^cite_note-686] On 12 March, a militant shot and injured an Israeli near the [Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_\(Israeli_settlement\)) settlement.[^cite_note-687] On 21 March, near the [Dolev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolev) settlement, a militant carried out a shooting attack targeting an Israeli bus and a [Magen David Adom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magen_David_Adom) ambulance, after which he engaged in an hours-long exchange of fire with the IDF; eventually, he was killed with a helicopter strike. The attacks resulted in several casualties.[^cite_note-688]

On 20 April, a Palestinian gunman opened fire at Israeli soldiers outside the [Homesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homesh) outpost and was killed by return fire.[^cite_note-689][^cite_note-690] On 30 April, two IDF reservists were injured by a bomb attack near the town of [Beita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beita,_Nablus) southeast of Nablus.[^cite_note-691]

On 7 May, a gunman opened fire on Israeli forces at the Reihan checkpoint, causing injuries. The same day, in Hebron, another militant attempted a car-ramming attack against IDF troops and then began stabbing soldiers.[^cite_note-692] On 14 May, a gunman shot two Israelis near the [Bruchin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruchin) settlement, one of whom was a pregnant woman who later died from her injuries.[^cite_note-693]

On 12 June, a gunman was killed after firing at Israeli troops at a checkpoint near the [Hermesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermesh) settlement.[^cite_note-694]

On 21 August, a gunman fired at Israeli settlers at the [Malachei HaShalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malachei_HaShalom) outpost, wounding one, and fled to the nearby Palestinian village of [Al-Mughayyir](18130182.md), [Ramallah Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramallah_Governorate). The IDF then subsequently launched a raid on the village, capturing the attacker after two days.[^cite_note-695][^cite_note-696]

On 8 September, gunmen conducted [a mass shooting in East Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Ramot_Junction_shooting), killing six Israelis.[^cite_note-697] On 12 September, a Palestinian assailant from [East Jerusalem](343508.md) carried out a stabbing attack in [Tzova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzova), northwest of Jerusalem, wounding two Israelis.[^cite_note-698] On 18 September, a Jordanian gunman shot and killed two Israelis at the Allenby Crossing.[^cite_note-699] On 28 September, a Palestinian assailant from Nablus carried out a vehicle-ramming attack against IDF soldiers at the [Jit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jit,_Qalqilya) junction near the Kedumim settlement, which resulted in the death of one soldier via [friendly fire](202484.md).[^cite_note-700] On 30 September, two Israeli teenagers were wounded in a vehicle-ramming attack at the [Al-Khader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Khader) junction, carried out by a Palestinian attacker who was shot dead at the scene.[^cite_note-701]

On 2 October, two militants attempted a vehicle-ramming and shooting attack against IDF soldiers at a checkpoint, without success; one was killed and the other was arrested.[^cite_note-702]

On 10 November, a militant shot at Israeli police officers near the [Beit Hagai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hagai) settlement; the exchange of fire resulted in his death.[^cite_note-703] On 18 November, two Palestinian attackers carried out a vehicle-ramming and stabbing attack at the [Gush Etzion Junction](28979076.md). One Israeli was killed and three others were wounded.[^cite_note-704]

On 1 December, a Palestinian attacker carried out a vehicle-ramming attack near the [Kiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Arba) settlement, which left an IDF soldier lightly injured.[^cite_note-\:03-705] On 2 December, two IDF soldiers were lightly wounded following a stabbing attack carried out near the [Ateret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ateret) settlement.[^cite_note-\:03-705] On 6 December, a vehicle-ramming attack at an IDF checkpoint in Hebron left one Israeli soldier lightly wounded.[^cite_note-706] On 14 December, IDF troops shot and killed a Palestinian who attempted to stab them at a junction near Hebron.[^cite_note-707] On 26 December, a Palestinian attacker from Qabatiya carried out two separate attacks in northern Israel that resulted in the deaths of two Israelis.[^cite_note-708]

### 2026

On 7 January, IDF troops operating near Beita were targeted by a Palestinian attacker throwing Molotov cocktails, but no injuries were reported.[^cite_note-\:17-709]

On 12 March, the IDF said two Palestinian gunmen opened fire at an army post, and then attempted a car-ramming before being shot dead near [Tapuah Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Tapuach).[^cite_note-710]

## Role of the Palestinian Authority

### PA conflict with militants

Further information: [Palestinian Authority–West Bank militias conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Authority%E2%80%93West_Bank_militias_conflict)

The [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) (PA), which is largely seen by Palestinians as ineffective and subjugated to the Israeli occupation,[^cite_note-711][^cite_note-shaheed20240731-712] has had a shared interest with Israel in suppressing militants even before the outbreak of the war.[^cite_note-713] Cooperation between the PA security forces and the IDF is reflected by the fact that the former have simply remained in their barracks during IDF raids[^cite_note-714][^cite_note-715] and have actively interfered with militants' defenses against those raids.[^cite_note-716][^cite_note-717][^cite_note-718][^cite_note-719]

The [July 2024 West Bank unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2024_West_Bank_unrest) directed against the Palestinian Authority was a major escalation in its conflict with the militants,[^cite_note-720] sparked by the [alleged arrest attempt of "Abu Shujaa"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/26_July_2024_Tulkarm_confrontations), the leader of the Tulkarm Brigade, on 26 July. Protestors and militants clashed with security forces in Tulkarm,[^cite_note-721][^cite_note-722][^cite_note-723] Jenin,[^cite_note-Adler-20240814-724][^cite_note-725][^cite_note-726][^cite_note-shaheed20240731-712] Bethlehem,[^cite_note-aljazeera20240727-727][^cite_note-728] Tubas,[^cite_note-aljazeera20240727-727] and Nablus.[^cite_note-shaheed20240731-712] By August, the unrest had been "contained", according to Tulkarm officials.[^cite_note-Adler-20240814-724]

The PA also launched notable operations [into Tubas in October 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Palestinian_Authority_operation_in_Tubas)[^cite_note-reuters20241024-59] and [into Jenin in December 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/December_2024_Palestinian_Authority_operation_in_Jenin).[^cite_note-729][^cite_note-730] PA forces began fighting alongside Israeli forces for the first time ever during Israel's 2025 "Iron Wall" operation.[^cite_note-middleeasteye202501222-731][^cite_note-732][^cite_note-\:israelblocks2-733]

### Other incidents

There has been one case during the war where there was an incident between the IDF and the Palestinian Authority, that being the shooting death of a Palestinian Authority [customs officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs_officer) during an IDF raid into Tubas on 24 July 2024.[^cite_note-aljazeera20240724-56][^cite_note-wafa20240724-57]

In the aftermath of the [3 October 2024 Tulkarm Camp airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Tulkarm_Camp_airstrike) by Israel, Laith Jaar, an [Al Jazeera journalist](30204052.md) who was covering the bombing was assaulted by a security officer, who threatened to shoot him. Jaar was subsequently arrested and detained by the security forces,[^cite_note-734][^cite_note-735] and released a day later.[^cite_note-736]

On 29 October 2024, Israeli settlers carried out a shooting attack against PA security forces near the [Havat Gilad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Havat_Gilad) settlement, injuring 2 officers; the IDF condemned the attack and began an investigation into it.[^cite_note-iran-update-october-29-2024-58]

On 10 July 2025, two PA police officers carried out a combined shooting and stabbing attack at the [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion) settlement bloc, which resulted in one Israeli casualty. Hamas praised the attack, while the [Palestinian Security Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Security_Services) were said to plan an internal investigation.[^cite_note-737]

## Imprisonment and checkpoints

See also: [Gaza war hostage crisis](75025451.md)

### Freedom of movement

A report by [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem) found that the Israeli military had severely cracked down on [Palestinians' freedom of movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_freedom_of_movement), stating it was using "its network of checkpoints to tighten supervision, setting up dozens of new checkpoints, blocking access from dozens of villages to main roads, and revoking all permits for Palestinians to enter Israel for work or other reasons".[^cite_note-738]

### Mass detentions

This section is an excerpt from [Mass detentions in the Gaza war § Palestinians in the West Bank and East Jerusalem](75234504.md#Palestinians_in_the_West_Bank_and_East_Jerusalem).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war\&action=edit)]

The International Committee of the Red Cross expressed "high concern" regarding "the sharp increase in \[the] number of arrests" in the occupied West Bank since October 7.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:23-739] Amnesty International has also criticized the "spike in arbitrary arrests" of Palestinians since October 7.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_Horrifying-740] Similarly, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported that the rate of arrests in the occupied West Bank has "more than doubled" since that date.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:13-741]

On October 17, Al Jazeera reported that nearly 700 people had been arrested in the West Bank and [occupied East Jerusalem](343508.md) since the outbreak of the war.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:11-742] By October 28, this number had climbed to 1,550, according to estimates by the Palestinian Prisoners Society.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:5-743] *[The Globe and Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Globe_and_Mail)* reported that Israel had advised the Palestinian Authority of the existence of 1,700 prisoners, but not their whereabouts, as of October 31.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:10-744] On November 6, *Al Jazeera* reported that 1,740 individuals had been arrested in overnight raids since the outbreak of the war; *BBC* reported the total number of arrestees at 2,150, according to the Palestinian Prisoners Society.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:14-745][^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:15-746] The *[Associated Press](18935732.md)* placed the number at 2,280 on November 8, citing the Palestinian Prisoners Society; on November 10, *[The Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Nation)* reported that the number was "at least 2,200".[^cite_note-747][^cite_note-748]

By November 8, the number of Palestinians held without charge or trial had increased from 1,319 to 2,070 since October 7, according to HaMoked.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_Horrifying-740] On November 15, *Mondoweiss* reported that arrests in the West Bank continued, with 54 Palestinians arrested the previous night in overnight raids.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:26-749] On November 28, the IDF told [*The* *Times of Israel*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel) that approximately 2,000 West Bank Palestinians had been arrested, while Palestinian monitoring groups reported 3,290 arrests.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:34-750][^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:33-751] On December 3, the Palestinian Prisoners Society reported 3,480 arrests, while the IDF reported 2,150 as of December 4 and 3,450 as of December 6.[^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:39-752][^cite_note-Mass_detentions_in_the_Gaza_war_\:40-753]

Al Jazeera reported that, as of January 10, 2024, nearly 6,000 Palestinians had been arrested in the West Bank since October 7, 2023.[^cite_note-754] An estimated 3,000 were being held under [administrative detention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_detention).[^cite_note-755]

Detainees arrested in [Deir Abu Mash'al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Abu_Mash'al), described the experience of being arrested by Israeli forces, stating that soldiers went door to door arresting people, blindfolding them, tying their hands, and taking them to an open building for interrogation.[^cite_note-756] Family members of detainees stated the Israeli army was unjustly arresting and interrogating people.[^cite_note-757]

Two directors and an employee from [The Freedom Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Freedom_Theatre) were arrested by the IDF, with one stating, "They treated us like animals. They are trying to hurt us in anyway they can."[^cite_note-758] One director, Mostafa Sheta, remained in custody and was believed to be held in the [Megiddo military prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megiddo_Prison) in northern Israel.[^cite_note-759] The [Royal Court Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Court_Theatre) responded to the reports by demanding for the release of the men.[^cite_note-760] On 22 February 2024, a spokesperson for [Birzeit University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birzeit_University) stated that Israeli forces had detained the undergraduate student council president, with more than sixty students arrested since 7 October.[^cite_note-761] In March 2024, verified footage showed Israeli soldiers arresting a group of Palestinians, tying them up with rope, and dragging them.[^cite_note-762] In July 2024, [PEN International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEN_International) condemned the detention of the president of PEN Palestine [Hanan Awwad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanan_Awwad) in East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-763]

In August 2024, Palestinian families in the West Bank reported hundreds of people were missing, with the Israeli military giving conflicting reports of their condition and whereabouts.[^cite_note-764]

After Palestinian-American teenager Mohammed Ibrahim was abducted from his family's West Bank home and imprisoned at Ofer prison, a coalition of 100 human and civil rights groups petitioned US Secretary of State Marco Rubio to secure his release.[^cite_note-765]

## Settler violence and depopulation of villages

### Displacement by military

The [UN Office for Humanitarian Affairs](1602284.md) reported Israeli forces demolished residential structures in [Furush Beit Dajan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Furush_Beit_Dajan) and [Deir Ballut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Ballut) on 25 December.[^cite_note-766] The UN reported 1,094 structures had been demolished and 2,127 Palestinians have been displaced in 2023.[^cite_note-767] On 27 December, UNOCHA stated that since 7 October, 1,208 people had been displaced due settler violence, 393 due to lacking Israeli building permits, 95 on punitive grounds, and 483 due to army demolitions.[^cite_note-Flash_Update_#77-768] On 15 January 2024, Israeli forces demolished two homes in [Qalqilya](851829.md), rendering 14 people homeless.[^cite_note-769] By 15 January, the number displaced due to army demolitions had grown to more than 600.[^cite_note-770]

In February 2024, a Palestinian activist whose family home in [East Jerusalem](343508.md) was destroyed by Israel stated, "Israeli authorities want to rid \[Jerusalem] of Palestinians and to change the makeup of the population."[^cite_note-771][^cite_note-772] A U.S. State Department spokesman said that the demolitions "damage Israel's standing in the world".[^cite_note-773] One man whose home was destroyed in [East Jerusalem](343508.md) stated he had not even been able to pack his belongings.[^cite_note-774] On 29 February, [Peace Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_Now) reported that the Israeli government had seized a large swath of land, threatening three Palestinian communities.[^cite_note-775] [UNOCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOCHA) reported that Israel had demolished the homes of 155 Palestinians in just January and February 2024.[^cite_note-776] In late-March 2024, UNOCHA reported that 1,640 people had been displaced in the West Bank and East Jerusalem since 7 October.[^cite_note-777] The [Colonization and Wall Resistance Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonization_and_Wall_Resistance_Commission_\(Palestine\)) stated 25 Bedouin communities had been displaced by Israel since 7 October.[^cite_note-778]

In May 2024, around 50 Bedouin family homes were destroyed in the [Negev desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negev_desert).[^cite_note-779] In July 2024, the [Israeli Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Supreme_Court) rejected the appeal of 11 East Jerusalem families against their evictions.[^cite_note-780] In 2024 alone, Israel seized 23.7sq km (9.15 sq miles) of Palestinian land in the West Bank, more than in the past 20 years combined.[^cite_note-781] In August 2024, the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) stated that 229 homes in East Jerusalem were demolished in 2023, the highest number on record.[^cite_note-782] Some families were forced to destroy their own homes.[^cite_note-783]

### Displacement by settler pogroms and intimidation

See also: [Pogroms § MENA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogroms#MENA)
